> help
Short walks, purchase lines and chats spend minutes within the hour; long acts finish the hour. Settings (standing, agency, rations) and queries take no time. Sleep keeps you in bed at least 4 hours (8 when you camp); wait to sleep on, or give any order (wake, drink, go) to get up.
Eat defaults to ONE serving; use eat hardtack 2 for two. Need effects settle once per hour; a partly spent hour uses its dominant activity.
Out on open ground with bedding, sleep makes camp where you stand; wait follows your lodging order, returning to a nearby paid or own bed when available.
look | signs | investigate <sign id> | actions [page] | go <place name/id> | talk <person> | <interaction> <person>
hire <employer> [daily|weekly] | quit | dismiss <worker>
buy <item> [qty] | sell <item> [qty] | eat <item> [servings] [method] | cook <item> [servings] [method]
drink | fill <canteen/kettle> | empty <canteen/kettle> | fetch water | meal | sleep | wake | rest | work | wait <Nh|Nd> | wait until <HH:00|dawn|morning|noon|dusk|night|tomorrow>
Springs, creeks, rivers and fords are water: drink raw there and fill your vessels (a litre weighs a kilo: a fill stops where you could no longer walk, and empty pours a vessel out); fetch water walks to the nearest.

1855-04-01 10:00 | FAIR | Kvale's Smithy
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $49.75; household $0.00. 42°F, dry.
Food 6.0 days; Medium, 32.7 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 754; Rest 767; Thirst 708; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
Mr. Kvale works the forge, and the fire is never let out between the first frost and the last. His hammer can be heard at Badger Ford, and the dogs along the street know it by heart. A tire platform stands in the yard, and the wagon wheels waiting on it are stacked like cheeses. Spring at Kvale's Smithy. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Johanne Kvale is here.
Here: Johanne Kvale (#7).
Conscience: The Sabbath must be kept (held for your order; you may: attend church)

  Ellis Wayfarer: “Ellis Wayfarer. I thought we ought to know each other's names.”
  Josiah Whitlock: “Josiah Whitlock. Neighbors ought to, whenever they meet.”

  Ellis Wayfarer: “May I introduce myself? Ellis Wayfarer.”
  Johanne Kvale: “Johanne Kvale. You may, and welcome.”
  Ellis Wayfarer: “What's the news, Mrs. Kvale?”
  Johanne Kvale: “Not a thing. Badger Hollow's as quiet as a churchyard.”
  Ellis Wayfarer: “Have you heard about the Know-Nothing lodge?”
  Johanne Kvale: “I've heard of it. I don't mean to know any more.”
  Ellis Wayfarer: “What do you count among your blessings?”
  Johanne Kvale: “A good stove. A better neighbor. A third thing that I'll think of.”

> go Tregear Farm
57 min left in this hour (3 spent).

You arrive at Tregear Farm (3 min on foot)
1855-04-01 11:00 | FAIR | Tregear Farm
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $49.75; household $0.00. 45°F, dry.
Food 6.0 days; Medium, 32.7 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 725; Rest 728; Thirst 652; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
On a bench of the west slope, facing the morning. The soil is silt loam, the best-drained on this side, and the water comes from a dug well on the bench. Cold air pools in the dooryard on still mornings, and the frost comes a week before it comes to the neighbors. Mr. and Mrs. Tregear work it with their son Bartholomew; they keep a single cow, a shoat in a pen, and no team to speak of. The fence is patched, and the root cellar is nearly through its winter stores. There is always a pot of strong tea on the hob, and a cup is poured before a visitor has said what brings him. Spring at Tregear Farm. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. The paths about Tregear Farm are quiet. As always here: ash gathers on the wrong side of the hearthstone. There is danger here; the ordinary rules feel uncertain. Iron at a threshold keeps small visitors out.
Here: no one else.
Conscience: The Sabbath must be kept (held for your order; you may: attend church)

1855-04-01 11:00 | FAIR | Ridge Baptist Church
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $49.75; household $0.00. 45°F, dry.
Food 6.0 days; Medium, 32.7 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 725; Rest 728; Thirst 652; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
A plain frame church; the bell was cast downriver. The congregation baptizes in the creek below the ford, and the whole district turns out to watch. The Sabbath school meets before the preaching. Spring at Ridge Baptist Church. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Hannah Whitlock, Josiah Whitlock, Jory Rowe are here.
Here: Hannah Whitlock (#1), Josiah Whitlock (#2), Jory Rowe (#3), Loveday Rowe (#4), Peran Rowe (#5, child), Tollef Kvale (#6); 14 more (inspect by name).

  Johanne Kvale: “Not a thing. Badger Hollow's as quiet as a churchyard.”
  Ellis Wayfarer: “Have you heard about the Know-Nothing lodge?”
  Johanne Kvale: “I've heard of it. I don't mean to know any more.”
  Ellis Wayfarer: “What do you count among your blessings?”
  Johanne Kvale: “A good stove. A better neighbor. A third thing that I'll think of.”

You arrive at Freeman Farm (2 min on foot)
1855-04-02 09:00 | FAIR | Freeman Farm
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $48.50; household $0.00. 39°F, dry.
Food 5.0 days; Medium, 31.4 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 942; Rest 922; Thirst 900; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
On a shelf under the east rim, with the afternoon sun full on the roof. The soil is light and sandy, quick to warm and quick to dry out, and the water comes from a well dug forty feet through sandstone. Mr. and Mrs. Freeman work it with their son Elijah; they keep two cows, a flock of sheep, and a team shared with a neighbor. A hired man, Peran Rausch, sleeps in the loft. Hollyhocks crowd the porch, grown from seed carried across the lake in a handkerchief. Spring at Freeman Farm. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Rebecca Freeman, Elijah Freeman, Peran Rausch are here.

  Ellis Wayfarer: “Do you think this place will change?”
  Sarah Bascom: “It already has. Last year there wasn't a church.”

  Wendell Bascom: “Then we'll fill it with the weather.”
  Sarah Bascom: “Anything in the post for you?”
  Wendell Bascom: “A bill and a tract. The tract's more welcome.”

1855-04-02 12:00 | FAIR | the Wagon Wheel Tavern
Ellis Wayfarer — at home, at leisure. Purse $48.50; household $0.00. 47°F, dry.
Food 5.0 days; Medium, 31.4 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 855; Rest 805; Thirst 732; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
Counter open; hours 6:00–23:00.
Board: bed $0.25 until noon; meal $0.20 (type meal).
Mr. Rowe keeps this frame house, with a long bar room below and beds above. Richard Pascoe, the keeper's brother-in-law, has the front room upstairs, and a survey chain hangs on a peg at the foot of the stairs. The porch roof sags at one corner, and a prop has stood under it so long it has put out roots. Spring at the Wagon Wheel Tavern. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Jory Rowe, Loveday Rowe, Peran Rowe are here. You remember: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. The question is still open; use signs.
Open sign: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. (investigate 25:0:dusk)
Open sign: The porridge bowl is licked clean on the outside. (investigate 22:1:dawn)
Here: Jory Rowe (#3), Loveday Rowe (#4, working), Peran Rowe (#5, child), Richard Pascoe (#24, working).
Conscience: The pantry is nearly bare: go to the store (held for your order; you may: call for the mail)

> journal
1855-04-01 00:00 Sunday, the 1st of April, 1855. The Sabbath under a full moon; the evening service will want no lantern.
1855-04-01 14:00 I helped Loveday Rowe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon. It cost me little and, I think, meant a good deal.
1855-04-01 14:00 Nils Dahl slighted me at the Wagon Wheel Tavern. I held my face still and counted to a hundred; it did not help.
1855-04-01 15:00 I drank a toast with Jory Rowe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon.
1855-04-01 16:00 Richard Pascoe made eyes at me at the Wagon Wheel Tavern on Sunday in the afternoon. I said something clever and have been trying to remember what.
1855-04-01 17:00 At the Wagon Wheel Tavern: The broom leans bristles upward against the shut door. I would like to know what made it.
1855-04-01 19:00 Loveday Rowe helped Jory Rowe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 00:00 Monday, the 2nd of April, 1855. The full moon, which the Norwegians watch and the Cornish do not discuss. Snow in the night.
1855-04-02 07:00 Loveday Rowe helped Peran Rowe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of her for it.
1855-04-02 07:00 Richard Pascoe helped Loveday Rowe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 08:00 I stopped to talk with Josiah Whitlock at Whitlock Mercantile on Monday in the forenoon. There was nothing new in it on either side.
1855-04-02 08:00 I put this down before memory alters it. Whitlock Mercantile: The porridge bowl is licked clean on the outside.
1855-04-02 09:00 I asked Rebecca Freeman for the news at Freeman Farm on Monday in the forenoon. She had nothing new for me, or nothing she would part with.
1855-04-02 12:00 I stopped to talk with Wendell Bascom at Bascom Parsonage on Monday at noon. There was nothing new in it on either side.
1855-04-02 12:00 Jory Rowe was in close talk with Richard Pascoe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern on Monday at noon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 12:00 Peran Rowe comforted Jory Rowe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern. I saw it, and thought the better of him for it.
1855-04-02 13:00 Jory Rowe stopped to talk at the Wagon Wheel Tavern on Monday in the afternoon. Old talk; I had it already, or there was none.
1855-04-02 13:00 Loveday Rowe was in close talk with Richard Pascoe at the Wagon Wheel Tavern on Monday in the afternoon. I would give a good deal to know the subject.
1855-04-02 14:00 Monday. Jory Rowe comforted me, unasked. There are people in this county who keep the word neighbor alive.
Owed to you: Josiah Whitlock owes you a favor.
Owed to you: Loveday Rowe owes you a favor.

Mrs. Vik, a widow, works it with her daughter Agnes; she keeps a good team, three cows and a flock of geese. A quarter-acre pile of fieldstones waits beside the pasture to become a wall. At the foot of the west slope, a field's width above the hay ground. The soil is heavy and rich, slow to dry in spring, and the water comes from a shallow well that turns brackish in a dry August. Mrs. Vik, a widow, works it with her daughter Agnes; she keeps a good team, three cows and a flock of geese. A quarter-acre pile of fieldstones waits beside the pasture to become a wall. Spring at Vik Farm. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Marta Vik is here.

1855-04-05 06:00 | FAIR | Kvale's Smithy
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $47.75; household $0.00. 33°F, dry.
Food 3.0 days; Medium, 27.2 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 711; Rest 784; Thirst 764; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
Mr. Kvale works the forge, and the fire is never let out between the first frost and the last. His hammer can be heard at Badger Ford, and the dogs along the street know it by heart. A tire platform stands in the yard, and the wagon wheels waiting on it are stacked like cheeses. Spring at Kvale's Smithy. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Tollef Kvale, Johanne Kvale, Lars Kvale are here.

1855-04-05 13:00 | FAIR | Tregear Farm
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $46.83; household $0.00. 51°F, dry.
Food 7.0 days; Medium, 29.5 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 977; Rest 473; Thirst 652; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
On a bench of the west slope, facing the morning. The soil is silt loam, the best-drained on this side, and the water comes from a dug well on the bench. Cold air pools in the dooryard on still mornings, and the frost comes a week before it comes to the neighbors. Mr. and Mrs. Tregear work it with their son Bartholomew; they keep a single cow, a shoat in a pen, and no team to speak of. The fence is patched, and the root cellar is nearly through its winter stores. There is always a pot of strong tea on the hob, and a cup is poured before a visitor has said what brings him. Spring at Tregear Farm. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Trystan Tregear, Jenifer Tregear, Bartholomew Tregear are here. As always here: ash gathers on the wrong side of the hearthstone. There is danger here; the ordinary rules feel uncertain. Iron at a threshold keeps small visitors out.
Here: Trystan Tregear (#15, working), Jenifer Tregear (#16), Bartholomew Tregear (#17, child).
Conscience: The pantry is nearly bare: go to the store (held for your order; you may: call for the mail)

1855-04-05 14:00 | FAIR | Tregear Farm
Ellis Wayfarer — at leisure. Purse $46.83; household $0.00. 52°F, dry.
Food 7.0 days; Medium, 29.5 kg.
Vitals: Hunger 948; Rest 434; Thirst 596; Warmth 1000
Health: Vigor 7/7; Wounds 0/12.
On a bench of the west slope, facing the morning. The soil is silt loam, the best-drained on this side, and the water comes from a dug well on the bench. Cold air pools in the dooryard on still mornings, and the frost comes a week before it comes to the neighbors. Mr. and Mrs. Tregear work it with their son Bartholomew; they keep a single cow, a shoat in a pen, and no team to speak of. The fence is patched, and the root cellar is nearly through its winter stores. There is always a pot of strong tea on the hob, and a cup is poured before a visitor has said what brings him. Spring at Tregear Farm. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground. Trystan Tregear, Jenifer Tregear are here. As always here: ash gathers on the wrong side of the hearthstone. There is danger here; the ordinary rules feel uncertain. Iron at a threshold keeps small visitors out.
Here: Trystan Tregear (#15, working), Jenifer Tregear (#16).

Mr. Kvale works the forge, and the fire is never let out between the first frost and the last. His hammer can be heard at Badger Ford, and the dogs along the street know it by heart. A tire platform stands in the yard, and the wagon wheels waiting on it are stacked like cheeses. Spring at Kvale's Smithy. Wheel ruts and footprints cross the softer ground.
